<div class="hlist"><ul><li><span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:046CupolaSPietro.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1063604349"><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Catholic_philosophy" title="Template:Catholic philosophy"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Catholic_philosophy" title="Template talk:Catholic philosophy"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Catholic_philosophy&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Francis de Sales</b>, <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Oratory" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation of the Oratory">C.O.</a>, <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Minims" title="Order of Minims">O.M.</a> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">François de Sales</i>; <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Francesco di Sales</i>; 21 August 1567&#160;&#8211;&#32;28 December 1622) was a <a href="/wiki/Savoyard_state" title="Savoyard state">Savoyard</a> <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a> prelate who served as <a href="/wiki/Bisohp_of_Geneva" class="mw-redirect" title="Bisohp of Geneva">Bishop of Geneva</a> and is a <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saint</a> of the Catholic Church. He became noted for his deep faith and his gentle approach to the religious divisions in his land resulting from the <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> <a href="/wiki/Reformation" title="Reformation">Reformation</a>. He is known also for his writings on the topic of <a href="/wiki/Spiritual_direction" title="Spiritual direction">spiritual direction</a> and spiritual formation, particularly the <i><a href="/wiki/Introduction_to_the_Devout_Life" title="Introduction to the Devout Life">Introduction to the Devout Life</a></i> and the <i>Treatise on the Love of God</i>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Life">Life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_years">Early years</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early years">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Francis de Sales was born two months premature on 21 August 1567 in the <a href="/wiki/Ch%C3%A2teau_de_Sales" title="Château de Sales">Château de Sales</a> into the noble Sales family of the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Savoy" title="Duchy of Savoy">Duchy of Savoy</a>, in what is today <a href="/wiki/Thorens-Gli%C3%A8res" title="Thorens-Glières">Thorens-Glières</a>, Haute-Savoie, France. His father was François de Sales, Lord of <a href="/wiki/Sales,_Haute-Savoie" title="Sales, Haute-Savoie">Sales</a>, and Novel, and by marriage, de Boisy. His mother was a noblewoman, Françoise de Sionnaz, the only child of the prominent magistrate, Melchior de Sionnaz, Seigneur de Vallières, de la Thuile, and de Boisy. This being one of the noblest families in Savoy, Francis's father was generally known as M. de Boisy.<sup id="cite_ref-Lear_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lear-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was baptized <b>Francis Bonaventura</b> after his godparents, François de la Fléchère and Damoiselle Bonaventure de Chevron-Villette, who was also his widowed, maternal grandmother.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> His father wanted him, the first of his six sons, to attend the best schools in preparation for a career as a <a href="/wiki/Magistrate" title="Magistrate">magistrate</a>. He, therefore, enjoyed a privileged education in the nearby town of <a href="/wiki/La_Roche-sur-Foron" title="La Roche-sur-Foron">La Roche-Sur-Foron</a>, and at the age of eight at the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Capuchin" title="Order of Friars Minor Capuchin">Capuchin</a> college in <a href="/wiki/Annecy" title="Annecy">Annecy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Pernin_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pernin-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education_and_self-consecration">Education and self-consecration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Education and self-consecration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Franz_von_Sales_12_Jahre.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Franz_von_Sales_12_Jahre.jpg/210px-Franz_von_Sales_12_Jahre.jpg" decoding="async" width="210" height="331" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Franz_von_Sales_12_Jahre.jpg/315px-Franz_von_Sales_12_Jahre.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Franz_von_Sales_12_Jahre.jpg/420px-Franz_von_Sales_12_Jahre.jpg 2x" data-file-width="614" data-file-height="969" /></a><figcaption>Francis de Sales age 12</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1578, de Sales went to the <a href="/wiki/Coll%C3%A8ge_de_Clermont" class="mw-redirect" title="Collège de Clermont">Collège de Clermont</a>, then a <a href="/wiki/Jesuit" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesuit">Jesuit</a> institution, to study <a href="/wiki/Rhetoric" title="Rhetoric">rhetoric</a> and humanities. On this first visit to Paris, he lived near the <a href="/wiki/Panth%C3%A9on" title="Panthéon">Église Sainte-Geneviève</a> with his three cousins.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> As a nobleman, he was accompanied by his servant and by a priest tutor, Abbé Déage. To please his father, he took lessons in the gentlemanly pursuits of riding, dancing, and fencing. De Sales is described as intelligent and handsome, tall and well built with blue-grey eyes, somewhat reserved and quiet, and a welcome guest in the homes of the nobility among whom his father had connections.
</p><p>In 1586, Francis de Sales attended a theological discussion about <a href="/wiki/Predestination" title="Predestination">predestination</a> that convinced him of his <a href="/wiki/Damnation" title="Damnation">damnation</a> to <a href="/wiki/Hell" title="Hell">hell</a>. A personal crisis of despair resulted. That conviction lasted through December 1586. His great despair made him physically ill and even bedridden for a time. Sometime in either late December or early January 1587, he visited the old parish of <a href="/wiki/Saint-%C3%89tienne-des-Gr%C3%A8s,_Paris" title="Saint-Étienne-des-Grès, Paris">Saint-Étienne-des-Grès, Paris</a>, where he prayed the "<a href="/wiki/Memorare" title="Memorare">Memorare</a>" before a famed statue of Our Lady of Good Deliverance, a <a href="/wiki/Black_Madonna" title="Black Madonna">Black Madonna</a>. He consecrated himself to the <a href="/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Blessed Virgin Mary">Blessed Virgin Mary</a> and decided to dedicate his life to <a href="/wiki/God" title="God">God</a> with a vow of chastity.<sup id="cite_ref-Pernin_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pernin-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> He then became a <a href="/wiki/Third_Order" class="mw-redirect" title="Third Order">tertiary</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Minim_(religious_order)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minim (religious order)">Minim Order</a>.
</p><p>De Sales ultimately concluded that God had good in store for him because "God is love", as the <a href="/wiki/First_Epistle_of_John" title="First Epistle of John">First Epistle of John</a> attests. This faithful devotion to God not only expelled his doubts but also influenced the rest of his life and his teachings. His way of teaching Catholic spirituality is often referred to as the Way of Divine Love, or the Devout Life, taken from a book he wrote of a similar name: <i><a href="/wiki/Introduction_to_the_Devout_Life" title="Introduction to the Devout Life">Introduction to the Devout Life</a></i>.
</p><p>De Sales completed his studies at <a href="/wiki/Coll%C3%A8ge_de_Clermont" class="mw-redirect" title="Collège de Clermont">Collège de Clermont</a> and enrolled at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Padua" title="University of Padua">University of Padua</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>, where he studied both law and theology.<sup id="cite_ref-Franmed_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franmed-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> He was accompanied by his twelve-year-old brother, Gallois, also a student in Padua. De Sales took <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Possevino" title="Antonio Possevino">Antonio Possevino</a>, a priest in <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">the Society of Jesus</a>, as his <a href="/wiki/Spiritual_director" class="mw-redirect" title="Spiritual director">spiritual director</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Return_to_Savoy">Return to Savoy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Return to Savoy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_St_Francis_de_Sales.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_St_Francis_de_Sales.jpg/210px-Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_St_Francis_de_Sales.jpg" decoding="async" width="210" height="308" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_St_Francis_de_Sales.jpg/315px-Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_St_Francis_de_Sales.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_St_Francis_de_Sales.jpg/420px-Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_St_Francis_de_Sales.jpg 2x" data-file-width="518" data-file-height="760" /></a><figcaption>St Francis de Sales, by Giovanni Battista Lucini</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1592, de Sales received his doctorate in law and theology and made up his mind to become a priest. He made a <a href="/wiki/Pilgrimage" title="Pilgrimage">pilgrimage</a> to <a href="/wiki/Loreto,_Marche" title="Loreto, Marche">Loreto</a>, Italy, famous for its <a href="/wiki/Basilica_della_Santa_Casa" title="Basilica della Santa Casa">Basilica Della Santa Casa</a> (Shrine of the Holy House) and then returned home to <a href="/wiki/Savoy" title="Savoy">Savoy</a>. As the eldest son and heir, he held the title of Seigneur de Villeroget. The Senate of <a href="/wiki/Chamb%C3%A9ry" title="Chambéry">Chambéry</a> admitted him as a lawyer. Meanwhile, his father secured various positions for Francis, including an appointment as a senator. His father also chose a wealthy noble heiress as his bride, but Francis refused to marry, preferring to stay focused on his chosen path. His father initially refused to accept that Francis had chosen the priesthood rather than fulfill his expectations with a political-military career. His cousin, Canon Louis de Sales, persuaded the Bishop of Geneva, Claude de Granier, to obtain for Francis the position of provost of the cathedral chapter of Geneva, a post in the patronage of the pope. It was the highest office in the diocese; M. de Boisy yielded. After signing over to his younger brother, Louis, his title and right of succession, Francis was ordained in 1593.<sup id="cite_ref-Franmed_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franmed-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Priest_and_provost">Priest and provost</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Priest and provost">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Because the Calvinists controlled Geneva, the bishop resided about twenty miles south, in <a href="/wiki/Annecy" title="Annecy">Annecy</a>. De Sales preached in the <a href="/wiki/Annecy_Cathedral" title="Annecy Cathedral">Cathedral of Annecy</a>, at parish churches and before confraternities. He was an effective speaker; his voice was deep and rich in tone and his speech somewhat slow and measured. His sermons were comparatively short and without the customary displays of erudition. He avoided controversy and focused on a particular point of duty, a specific virtue or the correction of some vice.<sup id="cite_ref-Lear_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lear-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> The cathedral chapter recommended that although he was only twenty-seven years of age, the provost be named Grand Penitentiary of the diocese, with the result that de Sales found himself taking many more confessions.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Chamblais">Chamblais</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Chamblais">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 1594, the Duke of Savoy requested the Bishop to send a missioner to Chamblais, an area that had been long held by the Swiss and only recently returned to Savoy. The task would be both difficult and dangerous, and the most qualified for the assignment was the provost. Despite his family's objections, de Sales readily accepted. Accompanied only by his cousin, the Canon Louis, they made their base the fortress of <a href="/wiki/Allinges" title="Allinges">Allinges</a> to which the Governor of the Province, Baron d'Hermance, insisted they return each night. Gradually they expanded their efforts, with de Sales concentrating on Thonon-les-Bains, which had become almost completely <a href="/wiki/Calvinist" class="mw-redirect" title="Calvinist">Calvinist</a>. He also attended the Savoyard soldiers garrisoned at Allinges.<sup id="cite_ref-Lear_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lear-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>They met great opposition from the Geneva ministers, who accused de Sales of being a sorcerer. He moved to Thonon, where he boarded with a widow, who on one occasion hid him from some armed men. More than once, he escaped death at the hands of assassins. His mother managed to send him some linen and money, which he distributed to the poor. A good deal of his religious instruction was handled individually and privately. It was at this time that Francis began writing pamphlets which were later collected and published as <i>The Catholic Controversy</i>. Gradually, the mission began to show some small success.
</p><p>In 1599 he was appointed <a href="/wiki/Coadjutor_bishop" title="Coadjutor bishop">coadjutor bishop</a> of Geneva. In 1602, he was sent on a diplomatic mission to <a href="/wiki/Henry_IV_of_France" title="Henry IV of France">Henry IV of France</a> to negotiate the restoration of Catholic worship in <a href="/wiki/Gex,_Ain" title="Gex, Ain">Gex</a>, a part of the diocese that had been returned to France.<sup id="cite_ref-salesian_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-salesian-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> He was invited to give the <a href="/wiki/Lent" title="Lent">Lenten</a> sermons at the <a href="/wiki/Chapel_Royal" title="Chapel Royal">Chapel Royal</a>. The morals at court reflected those of the King, which were notorious, but King Henry became personally attached to Francis and is said to have observed, "A rare bird, this Monsieur de Genève, he is devout and also learned; and not only devout and learned but at the same time a gentleman. A very rare combination".
</p><p>
While in Paris, he also met <a href="/wiki/Pierre_de_B%C3%A9rulle" title="Pierre de Bérulle">Cardinal Berulle</a> and <a href="/wiki/Marie_of_the_Incarnation_(Carmelite)" title="Marie of the Incarnation (Carmelite)">Madame Acarie</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> They consulted with him on matters such as the introduction of <a href="/wiki/Teresa_of_%C3%81vila" title="Teresa of Ávila">Teresa of Ávila</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Discalced_Carmelites" title="Discalced Carmelites">Carmelites</a> into France and plans for the reforming of monasteries and convents. He was consulted on matters of conscience by persons at court.</p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:CoA_Francis_de_Sales.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/CoA_Francis_de_Sales.svg/220px-CoA_Francis_de_Sales.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="235" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/CoA_Francis_de_Sales.svg/330px-CoA_Francis_de_Sales.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/CoA_Francis_de_Sales.svg/440px-CoA_Francis_de_Sales.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1150" data-file-height="1226" /></a><figcaption>Arms of St Francis de Sales</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Bishop_of_Geneva">Bishop of Geneva</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Bishop of Geneva">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1602, Bishop Granier died, and Sales was consecrated Bishop of <a href="/wiki/Geneva" title="Geneva">Geneva</a> by Vespasien Gribaldi, assisted by Thomas Pobel and Jacques Maistret, O.Carm., as co-consecrators. He resided in <a href="/wiki/Annecy" title="Annecy">Annecy</a> (now in France) because Geneva remained under Calvinist control and was therefore closed to him. His diocese became famous throughout Europe for its efficient organization, zealous clergy and well-instructed laity, an achievement in those days.<sup id="cite_ref-oblates_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oblates-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He worked closely with the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Capuchin" title="Order of Friars Minor Capuchin">Order of Friars Minor Capuchin</a>, which was very active in preaching the Catholic faith in his diocese. It  appreciated his cooperation so much that in 1617 it made him an official associate of the Order, the highest honour possible for a non-member. It is said that at <a href="/wiki/%C3%89vian-les-Bains" title="Évian-les-Bains">Evian</a>, on the south shore of <a href="/wiki/Lake_Geneva" title="Lake Geneva">Lake Geneva</a>, <a href="/wiki/Francis_of_Assisi" title="Francis of Assisi">Francis of Assisi</a> appeared to him and said: "You desire <a href="/wiki/Martyrdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Martyrdom">martyrdom</a>, just as I once longed for it. But, like me, you will not obtain it. You will have to become an instrument of your own martyrdom".<sup id="cite_ref-habig_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-habig-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> During his years as bishop, de Sales acquired a reputation as a spellbinding preacher and something of an <a href="/wiki/Ascetic" class="mw-redirect" title="Ascetic">ascetic</a>. His motto was, "He who preaches with love, preaches effectively". His goodness, patience and mildness became proverbial.<sup id="cite_ref-famvin_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-famvin-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Author">Author</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Author">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>These last qualities come through in de Sales' books, the most famous of which was <i>Introduction to the Devout Life</i>, which, unusually for the time, was written for <a href="/wiki/Laity" title="Laity">laypeople</a>, especially for women. In it, he counseled charity over penance as a means of progressing in the spiritual life. De Sales also left a  mystical work, the "Treatise on the Love of God", and many highly valued letters of <a href="/wiki/Spiritual_direction" title="Spiritual direction">spiritual direction</a>, including those with <a href="/wiki/Jane_Frances_de_Chantal" title="Jane Frances de Chantal">Jane Frances de Chantal</a> compiled in the <i>Letters of Spiritual Direction</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Founder">Founder</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Founder">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:San_Francisco_de_Sales,_de_Francisco_Bayeu_(Museo_del_Prado).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/San_Francisco_de_Sales%2C_de_Francisco_Bayeu_%28Museo_del_Prado%29.jpg/220px-San_Francisco_de_Sales%2C_de_Francisco_Bayeu_%28Museo_del_Prado%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="362" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/San_Francisco_de_Sales%2C_de_Francisco_Bayeu_%28Museo_del_Prado%29.jpg/330px-San_Francisco_de_Sales%2C_de_Francisco_Bayeu_%28Museo_del_Prado%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/San_Francisco_de_Sales%2C_de_Francisco_Bayeu_%28Museo_del_Prado%29.jpg/440px-San_Francisco_de_Sales%2C_de_Francisco_Bayeu_%28Museo_del_Prado%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1753" data-file-height="2881" /></a><figcaption>San Francisco de Sales</figcaption></figure>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Sales_et_Jeanne_de_Chantal.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Sales_et_Jeanne_de_Chantal.jpg/300px-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Sales_et_Jeanne_de_Chantal.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Sales_et_Jeanne_de_Chantal.jpg/450px-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Sales_et_Jeanne_de_Chantal.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Sales_et_Jeanne_de_Chantal.jpg/600px-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Sales_et_Jeanne_de_Chantal.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1160" data-file-height="580" /></a><figcaption><i>Francis de Sales and Jane Frances de Chantal, medal 1867</i></figcaption></figure>
<p>Along with Chantal, de Sales founded the women's <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Visitation_of_Holy_Mary" title="Order of the Visitation of Holy Mary">Order of the Visitation of Holy Mary</a> (Visitandines) in Annecy on 6 June 1610. Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Denis-Simon_de_Marquemont" title="Denis-Simon de Marquemont">Denis-Simon de Marquemont</a> required the order's members to maintain <a href="/wiki/Monastic" class="mw-redirect" title="Monastic">cloistered</a> lives.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sales also established a community of men, an <a href="/wiki/Oratory_of_St._Philip_Neri" class="mw-redirect" title="Oratory of St. Philip Neri">Oratory of St. Philip Neri</a>, at <a href="/wiki/Thonon-les-Bains" title="Thonon-les-Bains">Thonon-les-Bains</a>, with himself as the superior, or <a href="/wiki/Provost_(religion)#Roman_Catholic_Church" title="Provost (religion)">Provost</a>. That work, however, was crippled by his death, and that foundation soon died out.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In December 1622, de Sales was required to travel in the entourage of <a href="/wiki/Charles_Emmanuel_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles Emmanuel I">Charles Emmanuel I</a>, <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Savoy" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Savoy">Duke of Savoy</a>, for the Duke's Christmas tour of his domain. Upon arrival in Lyon, de Sales chose to stay in the gardener's hut at the Visitandine monastery in that city. While there he suffered a stroke, from which he died on 28 December 1622.<sup id="cite_ref-salesian_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-salesian-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Posthmous_veneration">Posthmous veneration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Posthmous veneration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>De Sales has been styled "the Gentleman Saint" because of his patience and gentleness.<sup id="cite_ref-habig_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-habig-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
Despite the resistance of the populace of Lyon to moving his remains from that city, he was buried on 24 January 1623 in the church of the Monastery of the Visitation in Annecy, which he had founded with Chantal, who was also buried there. Their remains were <a href="/wiki/Veneration" title="Veneration">venerated</a> there until the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution">French Revolution</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Many <a href="/wiki/Miracle" title="Miracle">miracles</a> have been reported at his <a href="/wiki/Shrine" title="Shrine">shrine</a>.
</p><p>His heart was kept in Lyon, in response to the popular demand of the citizens of the city to retain his remains. During the French Revolution, however, it was saved from the revolutionaries by being carried by the Visitation nuns from Lyons to <a href="/wiki/Venice" title="Venice">Venice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Pernin_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pernin-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>De Sales was <a href="/wiki/Beatified" class="mw-redirect" title="Beatified">beatified</a> in 1661 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Alexander_VII" title="Pope Alexander VII">Pope Alexander VII</a>, who then <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> him four years later. De Sales was declared a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">Doctor of the Church</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a> in 1877.<sup id="cite_ref-Crawley_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Crawley-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Roman Catholic Church celebrates de Sales' feast on 24 January, the day of his burial in Annecy in 1624.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> From 1666, when his <a href="/wiki/Feast_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast day">feast day</a> was inserted into the <a href="/wiki/General_Roman_Calendar" title="General Roman Calendar">General Roman Calendar</a> to its <a href="/wiki/Mysterii_Paschalis" title="Mysterii Paschalis">1969 revision</a>, it was celebrated on 29 January.
</p><p>Francis is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Festival_(Anglicanism)" title="Lesser Festival (Anglicanism)">Lesser Festival</a> on <a href="/wiki/24_January" class="mw-redirect" title="24 January">24 January</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> In 2022, Francis de Sales was officially added to the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Episcopal_Church)" title="Calendar of saints (Episcopal Church)">Episcopal Church liturgical calendar</a> with a feast day shared with <a href="/wiki/Jane_Frances_de_Chantal" title="Jane Frances de Chantal">Jane Frances de Chantal</a> on 12 December.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Patronage">Patronage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Patronage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In 1923, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> proclaimed him a patron of writers and journalists because de Sales made extensive use of broadsheets and books in spiritual direction and in his efforts to convert the Calvinists of the region.<sup id="cite_ref-Franmed_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franmed-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Sales developed a sign language to teach a deaf man about God. That has made him the patron saint of the deaf.<sup id="cite_ref-desalesu_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-desalesu-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Having been founded as one of the first non-cloistered group of sisters, after attempts had been with the <a href="/wiki/Visitation_Sisters" class="mw-redirect" title="Visitation Sisters">Visitation Sisters</a> founded by de Sales and de Chantal, the <a href="/wiki/Sisters_of_St._Joseph" title="Sisters of St. Joseph">Sisters of St. Joseph</a> (founded in Le Puys, France, in 1650) take de Sales as one of their patrons.
The Missionaries of St. Francis de Sales, founded by the <a href="/wiki/Abb%C3%A9" title="Abbé">Abbé</a> Pierre Mermier in 1838, were the first <a href="/wiki/Religious_congregation" title="Religious congregation">Religious congregation</a> to adopt his spirituality in the 19th century.
</p>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/Religious_institute" title="Religious institute">religious institute</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Salesians_of_Don_Bosco" title="Salesians of Don Bosco">Salesians of Don Bosco</a>, founded by <a href="/wiki/John_Bosco" title="John Bosco">John Bosco</a> in 1859 (approved by the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> in 1874), is also known as the Society of Saint Francis de Sales, and is placed under his patronage.</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Oblate_Sisters_of_St._Francis_de_Sales" title="Oblate Sisters of St. Francis de Sales">Oblate Sisters of St. Francis de Sales</a> were founded by <a href="/wiki/Leonie_Aviat" title="Leonie Aviat">Léonie Aviat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Louis_Brisson" title="Louis Brisson">Louis Brisson</a>, under the spiritual guidance of the <a href="/wiki/Marie_de_Sales_Chappuis" title="Marie de Sales Chappuis">Marie de Sales Chappuis</a> in 1866. The Oblates of St. Francis de Sales order for men was later founded by Brisson, also under the guidance of Marie de Sales, in 1875.<sup id="cite_ref-oblates_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oblates-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Oratory_of_Saint_Philip_Neri" title="Oratory of Saint Philip Neri">Congregation of the Oratory of St. Philip Neri</a> count him as one of their patrons, given his close association with St. Philip Neri's disciples and his founding of the Oratory in Thonon, France (now defunct), of which he was the first superior.</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Paulist_Fathers" title="Paulist Fathers">Paulist Fathers</a> in the United States count him.</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Congregations">Congregations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Congregations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the 19th century, his vision for religious communities was revived. Several religious institutes were founded during that period for men and women desiring to live out the spiritual path that  de Sales had developed.
</p>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/Missionaries_of_St._Francis_de_Sales" title="Missionaries of St. Francis de Sales">Missionaries of St. Francis de Sales</a> (MSFS), founded by the <a href="/wiki/Abb%C3%A9" title="Abbé">Abbé</a> Pierre Mermier in 1838, were the first <a href="/wiki/Religious_congregation" title="Religious congregation">congregation</a> to adopt his spirituality in the 19th century.</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Religious_institute" title="Religious institute">religious institute</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Salesians_of_Don_Bosco" title="Salesians of Don Bosco">Salesians of Don Bosco</a> (SDB), founded by <a href="/wiki/John_Bosco" title="John Bosco">John Bosco</a> in 1859 (approved by the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> in 1874), is also known as the Society of Saint Francis de Sales, and is placed under his patronage.</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Oblate_Sisters_of_St._Francis_de_Sales" title="Oblate Sisters of St. Francis de Sales">Oblate Sisters of St. Francis de Sales</a> (OSFS) were founded by <a href="/wiki/Leonie_Aviat" title="Leonie Aviat">Léonie Aviat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Louis_Brisson" title="Louis Brisson">Louis Brisson</a>, under the spiritual guidance of the <a href="/wiki/Marie_de_Sales_Chappuis" title="Marie de Sales Chappuis">Marie de Sales Chappuis</a> in 1866.</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Oblates_of_St._Francis_de_Sales" title="Oblates of St. Francis de Sales">Oblates of St. Francis de Sales</a> (OSFS) order for men was later founded by Brisson, also under the guidance of Marie de Sales, in 1875.<sup id="cite_ref-oblates_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oblates-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Paulist_Fathers" title="Paulist Fathers">Paulist Fathers</a> in the United States count him as one of their patrons.</li></ul>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_(St._Louis,_Missouri)_-_St._Francis_de_Sales_mosaic_(perspective_corrected).png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Saint_Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_%28St._Louis%2C_Missouri%29_-_St._Francis_de_Sales_mosaic_%28perspective_corrected%29.png/220px-Saint_Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_%28St._Louis%2C_Missouri%29_-_St._Francis_de_Sales_mosaic_%28perspective_corrected%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="221" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Saint_Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_%28St._Louis%2C_Missouri%29_-_St._Francis_de_Sales_mosaic_%28perspective_corrected%29.png/330px-Saint_Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_%28St._Louis%2C_Missouri%29_-_St._Francis_de_Sales_mosaic_%28perspective_corrected%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Saint_Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_%28St._Louis%2C_Missouri%29_-_St._Francis_de_Sales_mosaic_%28perspective_corrected%29.png/440px-Saint_Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_%28St._Louis%2C_Missouri%29_-_St._Francis_de_Sales_mosaic_%28perspective_corrected%29.png 2x" data-file-width="3824" data-file-height="3840" /></a><figcaption>Mosaic of Sales on the exterior of <a href="/wiki/St._Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_(St._Louis)" title="St. Francis de Sales Oratory (St. Louis)">St. Francis de Sales Oratory</a> in St. Louis, Missouri</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Institute_of_Christ_the_King_Sovereign_Priest" title="Institute of Christ the King Sovereign Priest">Institute of Christ the King Sovereign Priest</a>, a society of priests founded in the 20th century, also has Francis de Sales as one of their three primary patrons. One of the major <a href="/wiki/Apostolates" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolates">apostolates</a> of the Institute in the United States is the <a href="/wiki/St._Francis_de_Sales_Oratory_(St._Louis)" title="St. Francis de Sales Oratory (St. Louis)">Oratory of St. Francis de Sales</a> in <a href="/wiki/St._Louis,_Missouri" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Louis, Missouri">St. Louis, Missouri</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Influence_on_other_saints">Influence on other saints</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Influence on other saints">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Vincent_de_Paul" title="Vincent de Paul">Vincent de Paul</a> met de Sales in Paris in 1618 or 1619, whose spirituality and writings, especially <i>An Introduction to the Devout Life</i>, and <i>Treatise on the Love of God</i>, were to have a profound influence on Vincent.<sup id="cite_ref-famvin_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-famvin-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His writings on the perfections of the heart of Mary as the model of love for God influenced <a href="/wiki/John_Eudes" title="John Eudes">John Eudes</a> to develop the devotion to the <a href="/wiki/Hearts_of_Jesus_and_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Hearts of Jesus and Mary">Hearts of Jesus and Mary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Namesakes">Namesakes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_de_Sales&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Namesakes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
